## Rhythm Seasons Step 1: model and synthetic persistence (2026-10-25)

**Scope and workspace:** implemented the model, strict validation, schema migration, editor integration, and isolated synthetic-storage recovery only. No Seasons UI, historical-edit preview, deletion, Undo, score snapshot, legacy edit, or shared-browser data migration was performed. The worktree is `/Users/ice/dev/copilot-worktrees/steveotto_tend/gh11-tend2-foundation`, branch `tend2-rhythm-scheduling`, HEAD `e095ab1f1be188dfed06e88c46a0dfdf2816e6b8`, upstream `origin/tend2-rhythm-scheduling`. Changes remain uncommitted.

**Clarified schedule-change policy:** Seasons are schedule changes, not suggested stages. No label or note is generated; an initial schedule-history entry is internal and is not presented as a user-created named Season. Before effective dated completion history exists, an explicit schedule change updates schedule snapshots in the existing history without adding a boundary. Once dated history exists, a scoring-relevant change preserves earlier snapshots and appends a new Season at the effective local date; same-day changes replace the active snapshot while retaining its stable identity. Cosmetic edits do not change schedule history. The editor receives the effective dated-history fact from resolved synthetic completion records. This supersedes the previous editor behavior that appended a segment for every scoring-relevant schedule edit: the no-history branch now updates the initial schedule without introducing an additional boundary. Migration still preserves every trusted prior boundary even when no dated records exist; it never collapses history merely because records are absent. A prior dated record that has been deleted is no longer present in the synthetic history used by the editor; no separate “history once existed” marker was added.

**Authoritative schema and intervals:** `RhythmScoringMetadata.scheduleHistory` remains the sole source of truth; no parallel Seasons array was added. The active preview-envelope schema is now **v9**. Its schedule segments carry stable `id`, inclusive local `startDate`, structured `schedule`, optional user `name` (80-character maximum) and `note` (1,000-character maximum), and one of the six explicit palette tokens (`fern`, `sage`, `sky`, `ocean`, `plum`, `clay`). IDs are deterministic as `season:<rhythm-id>:<start-date>` and the initial palette token is deterministically selected from that ID; both are serialized and retained on ordinary hydration/export/import. The model rejects empty/oversized IDs, duplicate IDs or start dates, invalid dates/schedules, unknown fields/tokens, and oversized optional text. Segments are strictly chronological; each starts inclusively and ends exclusively at the next start. Inclusive display end dates are derived in UTC calendar arithmetic and are not stored as a second segment boundary. Schedule-level end dates remain in the structured schedule snapshot. Tracking start and scoring timezone remain separate metadata.

**Migration and recovery:** v7 and v8 schedule-history records are validated with the prior strict inclusive-boundary validator and migrated in memory to v9 Seasons. Their schedules and trustworthy start boundaries are retained; v8 inclusive segment ends are derived from the next start after validating contiguity. Migration IDs/palette tokens are deterministic. Existing v9 IDs and metadata are never regenerated during hydration. Rhythms without trustworthy scoring history gain no fabricated schedule history, tracking date, label, note, weekday, or schedule. Hydration does not write. Before a first explicit replacement of a valid v8 active envelope, the exact source bytes are preserved at `tend2:synthetic-preview:v8-recovery`; an existing differing recovery copy, active-snapshot conflict, or recovery write failure blocks replacement. The v8 recovery copy is exposed through the existing generic backup controls. The existing compare-before-write stale snapshot check remains; it is not an atomic cross-tab transaction. Malformed/future envelopes remain blocked from silent replacement and retain raw-download recovery paths.

**Shared consumers and checks:** `rhythm-scoring.ts` and `rhythm-occurrences.ts` now use the same pure date-to-Season selector, and history display ends use its pure derived-date helper. The shared editor creates/replaces/appends metadata through `createRhythmSeason`; no parallel recurrence or season-specific card/editor was introduced. Synthetic tests cover chronological lookup and leap-day boundaries, stable identity/palette, strict validation and text limits, pre-log edits, logged-history edits, same-day replacement, cosmetic edits, v7/v8 migration, hydration without writes, exact-byte v8 recovery, recovery conflict/failure blocking, export/import identity, unrelated goal/completion preservation, and scoring-result/evidence parity for a migrated fixture.

Fresh checks for this Step 1 implementation: `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed. `cd foundation-preview && npm test` passed: reader **7/7**, component/domain Vitest **290/290 across 17 files**, legacy **175/175** (**472 total tests**). `cd foundation-preview && npm run build` passed (80 modules transformed); it emitted the existing warning `/tend.css doesn't exist at build time, it will remain unchanged to be resolved at runtime`. `git diff --check` passed. The shared browser storage was deliberately not read or migrated for this task; persistence and recovery were verified with isolated in-memory synthetic fixtures. No new dependency or lockfile was added.

**Remaining scope and Step 2 proposal:** no Seasons UI exists yet. The next approved step can add a schedule-history view and optional Season naming during an explicit schedule change that preserves logged history, reusing the existing shared schedule controls. Gate Season presentation until such a logged-history-preserving change exists; identify unlabeled Seasons from their schedule and dates. Do not add historical edit previews, deletion, Undo, or score snapshots until separately approved.

## Rhythm Seasons Step 2: schedule confirmation and read-only history (2026-10-25)

**Scope:** added an optional blank-by-default Season label during a schedule change that preserves dated completion history, plus the collapsed, read-only Seasons history in the shared Rhythm history modal. No historical boundary editing, deletion, Undo, color picker, future-start planning, scoring change, or Today card redesign was added.

**Persistence and schedule-change behavior:** the Step 1 `RhythmScoringMetadata.scheduleHistory` remains authoritative; there is no parallel Seasons collection. The envelope is now schema **v10**, adding `rhythmHistoryIds` as a sticky record that a Rhythm has had dated completion history. New dated records add the marker, deleting the last dated record does not remove it, and a v9 migration infers markers only from actual dated completion records. Hydration remains read-only. Before the first explicit v9 replacement, the exact source bytes are retained under `tend2:synthetic-preview:v9-recovery`; recovery conflicts/failures and stale active snapshots continue to block replacement. Export/import and reload retain marker, stable Season IDs, labels, palette tokens, schedules, and logs.

The shared editor compares normalized structured schedule values. A change before any dated history updates the current/initial schedule snapshot without adding a Season boundary or showing confirmation. With dated history, a changed structured schedule opens the existing `MoneyGoalDialog` with previous → new schedule, “Starting today,” the earlier-log explanation, and an optional 80-character label. Confirm uses the shared `saveRhythmDraft` command and one whole-envelope `writePreview`; a persistence/validation error leaves the editor draft available and reports the error. Cancel, X, and Escape save nothing and return focus to Save. Same-day changes replace that day’s active segment rather than creating duplicate boundaries; cosmetic and unchanged edits do not confirm. Existing dated logs and their records remain untouched.

**Read-only Seasons history:** the shared `RhythmHistoryView` shows “Seasons (N)” immediately before All completion history only when more than the initial schedule segment exists. It is collapsed by default and uses a real expanded-controls disclosure. Expanded content contains the quiet introduction, a compact oldest-to-newest elapsed-time timeline with stable palette tokens and accessible labels, the current schedule/date range, and newest-first expandable earlier entries with full schedule and optional note. Dates are derived from adjacent inclusive-start boundaries; duration is calendar-date based through today. Color is not the sole identifier, and no score/performance summaries or edit actions are shown. Archived/read-only behavior is unchanged.

The change was then confirmed with the optional label “Morning connection.” After reload, the modal showed `Seasons (2)`, current Monday schedule `Oct 25 – Oct 25`, and earlier Daily schedule `Sep 25 – Oct 24`; one logged moment remained, with no duplicate or edited completion. The expanded view and older-schedule details were inspected visually and with accessible text. The exact original 1,079-byte preview envelope was restored, reloaded, and checked: it contains no temporary Rhythm and `window.name` returned to its prior empty value. No live account or legacy state was read or changed.

**Reuse and duplication review:** `RhythmEditor` remains the single Add/Edit form and reuses `MoneyGoalDialog`, `RhythmScheduleFields`, and `StyledSelect`; the confirmation is nested modal content, not a second editor. `RhythmDetailsDialog` and `RhythmHistoryView` remain shared across Today, Marriage, and archived detail/history contexts. `RhythmHistoryView` composes the one `SeasonsHistory` rendering path and the existing schedule formatter; no category-specific Seasons card or dialog copies were added. ## Rhythm Seasons Step 4: deletion with impact review and Undo (2026-10-25)

**Scope and domain behavior:** a pure typed deletion command removes only an eligible non-first structured Season boundary. Its predecessor extends through the deleted range and stops at the next surviving boundary; deleting the current Season also synchronizes the rhythm's current schedule and saved time metadata. Unknown-history entries, the first Season, invalid intervals, and boundaries whose predecessor has an explicit end date are rejected rather than guessed or silently rewritten. Surviving Season IDs, labels, notes, palette tokens, and all dated logs remain unchanged. Schema v11 and its existing storage, recovery, import/export, and stale-write paths are reused; no new schema or parallel history store was introduced.

**Impact review and safe Undo:** the single confirmation dialog presents the removed range, before/after schedule boundaries, preserved-log count, and eligible/completed and Consistency/On Track/Health evidence from the same `selectRhythmScore` selector used for the saved state. “Delete season” is explicit. Cancel, X, and Escape do not save, and focus returns to the opener. Persistence failures retain the original history and report an error. Successful deletion exposes a persistent-in-dialog status and Undo action; after a two-Season current deletion leaves only one Season, the Seasons disclosure remains visible for that Undo. Undo stores only the removed segment and expected schedule state, restores original metadata when that state still matches, preserves unrelated log edits, and refuses with an explanation after a conflicting schedule edit. Undo is in-memory only and does not survive reload; deletion itself is durable. Cross-tab atomicity is limited to the existing adapter guarantees.

**Isolated browser verification:** the shared `http://127.0.0.1:4173/app-preview.html` storage was not used. On strict port 4174, a synthetic three-Season/four-log fixture was used for middle-deletion preview/cancel/confirm/Undo. The rendered middle-delete preview showed Consistency **25 → 14**, On Track **80 → 40**, Health **39 → 21**; the saved result matched the preview, all four log IDs remained, Cancel left the envelope unchanged, and Undo restored the original boundaries and focus. A current-Season deletion from three segments was reloaded and verified with its predecessor schedule/time active and Undo absent, as expected after reload. In a further two-Season case, deleting the current Thursday segment showed Consistency **36 → 17** (11 → 24 eligible, 4 completed), On Track **100 → 80**, Health **52 → 33**, and two preserved logs in the affected interval. The result showed `Seasons (1)` with Undo. After a real rhythm-editor change from Daily to Monday, stale Undo refused to restore and displayed “Schedule history changed after deletion. This Season cannot be safely restored without overwriting those changes.” The Monday schedule and all four logs remained unchanged. The original 1,079-byte synthetic envelope was restored exactly on port 4174 and its task-owned Vite server was stopped; shared port 4173 remains untouched.

**Automated coverage and reuse:** tests cover middle/current deletion, first/unknown and interval restrictions, predecessor/next-boundary behavior, stable IDs/colors, exact log/Note preservation, preview/result scoring parity, current schedule/time synchronization, cancel and persistence failure, metadata restoration, unrelated log edits, stale Undo rejection, reload/export/import, focus, and the one-Season Undo visibility regression. `RhythmSeasonImpact` is shared by deletion and historical correction; `RhythmSeasonDeleteDialog` is the focused confirmation surface; `RhythmHistoryView` composes the existing `SeasonsHistory` and owns the status/focus behavior; `App` coordinates the validated storage update and transient undo snapshot. The added dialog is separate from the correction dialog because deletion has distinct confirmation and Undo semantics, while score presentation remains shared. There is no static architecture checker; runtime/component contracts do not replace screen-reader or cross-tab testing.

## Rhythm period-based scoring (2026-10-26)

**Scope and approved Season-boundary decision:** implemented period-based completion matching in the shared pure scoring/occurrence path. The current branch is `tend2-rhythm-scheduling` at `41ddbf1908bdf1516e9fc51880780f6ae05f1e1b`; existing dirty work was preserved and remains uncommitted. A mid-period schedule/Season change may create separate due occurrences when each Season defines a due date. Each occurrence window is clipped to its own Season, tracking start, and schedule end; a completion is matched only to its own Season and cannot cross that boundary. Distinct due dates can therefore produce distinct occurrences within one calendar period, but a log in one Season cannot satisfy another Season's occurrence.

**Matching and score rules:** single-weekday Weekly uses Monday–Sunday calendar weeks. Monthly uses calendar months, Quarterly calendar quarters, and Yearly calendar years. The existing due weekday/date/pattern still defines the occurrence. A completion anywhere in the occurrence's clipped eligible period (early, on its due date, or late) satisfies it. All qualifying logs in that same window are retained as evidence/history, assigned to that occurrence, and yield only one Consistency credit; a log cannot be assigned twice. A new empty occurrence is excluded from Consistency until its due date has ended, while a completed occurrence is included even when completed early. Daily and multi-day Weekly remain date-specific; Daily's completed-today inclusion and pending-today exclusion remain unchanged. Existing lookback windows and tracking-start behavior are retained, and no occurrence is manufactured before tracking/Season start.

On Track consumes the same occurrence evidence. An unsatisfied occurrence becomes a 10-point miss only after its eligible window ends; its qualifying completion clears the missed streak at the completion's effective date and restores the existing score to 100. There is no separate lateness penalty, extra logs do not create another reset or deduction, and the existing 100-point floor remains. Current/pending periods do not create overdue deductions. The existing schedule-change event remains an explanation in the history but does not independently reset the missed streak. Health continues to use the shared established 75% Consistency / 25% On Track blend, rounding, and unavailable behavior. Actual completion-chart totals and Logged moments continue to count records, not credited occurrences.

**Examples and boundaries:** with Monthly due on Oct 1, tracking from Oct 1, and records on Oct 17, 18, and 20, the exact-date baseline has 1 eligible occurrence, 0 credited, and 1 overdue by Oct 21; the updated selector has 1 eligible, 1 credited, and 0 overdue, while keeping all 3 records. With Monthly due Oct 20, an Oct 17 log similarly yields 1 eligible, 1 credited, and 0 overdue after Oct 20. An October log cannot satisfy November. A tracking start on Oct 17 cannot manufacture the Oct 1 occurrence, so that case has no eligible October occurrence. In a mid-October Season change from a Monthly due-on-1 schedule ending Oct 14 to due-on-20 starting Oct 15, the validated evidence is 2 eligible due occurrences, 1 credited by a new-Season log, and 0 overdue as of Oct 21; the older due date remains unsatisfied but its clipped window ends Oct 14 and is not yet overdue until the as-of date is after that end.

**Custom and Select Days limitation:** all Custom intervals retain the existing exact-date recurrence matching; Custom Consistency remains unavailable because no lookback policy has been approved. No Custom branch was generalized to ordinary calendar periods; in particular, anchored “every N weeks/months/years” cannot be treated as an ordinary calendar week/month/year without changing its recurrence semantics. Descriptive cadence (such as “3 times a week”) still does not infer a structured schedule. The stored union represents both Weekly and Select Days as `{ frequency: 'weekly', weekdays: [...] }`. Multi-day schedules remain date-specific, but a one-day Select Days selection is indistinguishable from a single-weekday Weekly schedule and is period-based under the current serialized model. No schema or completion-record rewrite was introduced; resolving that single-day ambiguity requires a separately approved representation decision.

**Shared implementation and reuse:** `rhythm-occurrences.ts` supplies pure calendar-period bounds, period classification, and correction-warning eligibility; `rhythm-scoring.ts` builds the common clipped occurrence/record evidence used for Consistency and On Track. `rhythm-score-presentation.ts` carries that evidence to history explanations; `RhythmHistoryView.tsx` presents concise early/eligible-period/satisfied explanations while keeping all logs; `RhythmSeasonCorrectionDialog.tsx` and `RhythmSeasonImpact.tsx` reuse the same eligibility/scoring path for correction previews. `RhythmSeasonDeleteDialog` and correction still use shared `RhythmSeasonImpact`; no parallel component scoring implementation was added. A history component regression checks the October 17/18/20 example: one credited monthly occurrence, the period eligibility explanation, the earliest satisfying completion, and all 3 Logged moments.

**Browser and data-integrity evidence:** port 4174 remained available. The isolated `localhost:4174` draft inspection covered Weekly Monday, Monthly due on the 20th, Quarterly due on the 20th, and Yearly due on the 20th; each had one eligible and one completed occurrence from the synthetic October logs. The Monthly due-on-the-1st draft had no eligible occurrence because its tracking boundary was Oct 17, confirming the boundary safeguard rather than silently backfilling. Drafts were canceled without saving. On the existing `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html` origin, the synthetic-preview key remained present at 1,079 characters with SHA-256 `9c56e4aefb8ba133c7bc533f5da4958862f66db7d817417e2c88ad1cdf19cb4a`, matching the pre-inspection fingerprint exactly. The visible page returned to Today; no records, preview envelope, live account, or database were changed.

**Fresh verification (2026-10-26):** `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed. `cd foundation-preview && npm run test:reader` passed **7/7**. `cd foundation-preview && npm run test:components` passed **355/355 across 21 files**, including the 62/62 focused `rhythm-history.component.test.tsx` run. `cd foundation-preview && npm run test:legacy` passed **175/175**. All requested suites total **537 passing tests**. `cd foundation-preview && npm run build` passed (85 modules transformed); Vite emitted the existing `/tend.css doesn't exist at build time; it will remain unchanged to be resolved at runtime` warning. `git diff --check` passed before this handoff update; it is rerun after the update. Build output remains under the repository's ignored `foundation-preview/dist/`.

**Reuse map and limits:** `rhythm-occurrences.ts` → scoring, correction-warning eligibility, and schedule-period helpers; `rhythm-scoring.ts` → Consistency, On Track, Health, history evidence, and Season impact previews; `RhythmHistoryView` → Today, Marriage, and archived/detail history. Browser drafts covered representative frequencies but did not edit or save the shared preview. The one-day Select Days serialization ambiguity and Custom Consistency lookback policy remain open constraints; no unsupported Custom-period behavior is claimed.

## Follow-up: investigate missing Seasons after schedule change (2026-10-25)

**Observed browser state:** the browser canvas remained at `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html`, but no process was listening on port 4174 and an HTTP request failed. The preceding task-owned server was Vite launched from this checkout’s `foundation-preview` directory with `--host 127.0.0.1 --port 4174 --strictPort`; it was stopped after cleanup. Port 4173 was also not listening; it was not started or substituted. The checkout was `tend2-rhythm-scheduling` at `bf735f16af2e87c270ca28f4f300431660a6d254`. The only pre-existing working-tree change was this handoff file. Reading only `localStorage["tend2:synthetic-preview"]` in the existing 4174-origin page found the 1,079-byte schema-v11 envelope with **0 saved rhythms and 0 dated completions**. No rhythm ID, schedule, log, or Season boundary can be attributed to the current browser state. No browser data or code was changed. The empty envelope is consistent with the exact restoration recorded above; therefore the actual current rhythm cannot be inspected and the browser workflow answers below cannot be inferred from this empty envelope.

**Implementation trace and same-day behavior:** when a saved rhythm has a structured schedule, the editor compares the draft schedule to the existing one; if they differ and dated history exists, clicking **Save Rhythm** opens the `Change your schedule` dialog. It displays the old → new schedule, **Starting today**, **Earlier logs keep their previous schedule**, and an optional label; **Confirm change** calls the shared save path. `App.saveRhythm` passes the date-history flag and local date/time zone to `saveRhythmDraft`, then writes the resulting envelope through the normal guarded `writePreview` adapter. The pure command appends a new Season when the effective local date is later than the active segment’s start date. When the active segment already starts on that same local date, it deliberately replaces that segment’s schedule/name rather than creating a duplicate date boundary. Consequently, if the entire history starts on the change date, even a rhythm with dated logs can end with only one schedule-history segment and the disclosure remains hidden. The current disclosure condition is `scheduleHistory.length > 1 || matching transient Undo`; labels and log counts do not affect it. This same-day replacement behavior is explicitly covered by the existing `rhythm-editor.component.test.tsx` test. Changing it to preserve multiple same-day schedules would require a policy/model for ordering changes within one date; the existing date-only Season intervals cannot represent two same-day boundaries. No policy or implementation change was made.

**Prior browser evidence, not the current saved state:** the preceding isolated acceptance run recorded a synthetic rhythm with three logs, an Oct 1 initial segment, and schedule changes effective Oct 25. Its Save confirmation was shown and, after confirmation, history held the Oct 1 and Oct 25 boundaries; `Seasons (2)` rendered in the details/history dialog above All completion history. A same-day Oct 25 update replaced the active Oct 25 segment but did not remove the earlier Oct 1 segment. The final deletion later removed the Oct 25 segment, leaving one Oct 1 segment; that fixture was then restored to the original empty envelope and its 4174 server stopped. Thus the documented sequence does not show a schedule edit itself hiding an existing earlier boundary. It also cannot establish what the user’s current rhythm did, whether its changes were same-day, or what appears after reloading a live 4174 server.

**Disposition:** no confirmed defect in the user’s actual current rhythm could be established because that rhythm and its history are absent from the isolated envelope. The code does have the documented same-day collapse behavior, whose consequence is a one-segment history and no Seasons disclosure when the history began that day. The existing test validates replacement, not the rendered disclosure for that case. Do not treat that as the proven cause of the user’s missing disclosure without the rhythm’s saved v11 record. No source changes or tests were run; the worktree data and browser envelope were preserved. The confirmation’s exact location is the dialog opened by Save Rhythm, headed **Change your schedule**, with the **Confirm change** button described above. Fresh `git diff --check` was run after this documentation update.

## Same-day schedule change with backdated logs (2026-10-25)

**Reproduction and cause:** the isolated v11 envelope at `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html` was initially empty. Using the actual preview UI, created **“Seasons same-day boundary reproduction”** (`user-rhythm-b6dfbc84-dd52-48cb-8c7c-3d52b14f275f`) with a Monday schedule, logged moments dated Oct 5, 12 and 19, 2026, then changed its schedule on Oct 25 through Edit Rhythm → Save Rhythm → **Change your schedule** → **Confirm change**. Before the fix, the same-day replacement left only the Oct 25 boundary, so the one-segment history did not satisfy the Seasons disclosure condition; the older logs had no honest schedule interval. This is distinct from replacing a same-day segment when an earlier known Season already exists: that earlier boundary remains visible. The date-only model cannot infer an earlier schedule from those backdated records.

**Fix and observed result:** the shared `saveRhythmDraft` command now accepts the earliest associated dated-completion date. If it precedes the first known scheduled Season, the command records an unknown segment for that interval instead of inventing a cadence; `App.saveRhythm` derives the date from dated completions and active completion overrides. Existing known boundaries are retained. The focused domain regression covers the reported same-day change and asserts the unknown Oct 5 segment plus the known Oct 25 schedule. In the preview, changed the reproduction rhythm back to Monday on Oct 25. Its history displayed **Seasons (2)** above **All completion history**: **Every Mon · Current** for Oct 25 and **Earlier schedule needs details** for Oct 5–24, with the existing **Set earlier schedule** repair action. The earlier schedule is deliberately unavailable until repaired; no weekday was inferred for Oct 5–24.

**Reload and preservation:** reloaded `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html`, reopened the rhythm history, and confirmed **Seasons (2)** and both entries remained visible above All completion history. A read-only inspection of only `localStorage["tend2:synthetic-preview"]` confirmed the same two boundaries, current Monday schedule, and all three original completion IDs, effective dates and `13:22` local times. The test rhythm and records remain in the isolated preview for review; the envelope is 2,745 bytes. Port 4174 is still served by this checkout’s Vite process (PID 66714; HTTP 200). Port 4173 was not used or changed.

**Checks and limits:** after the code change, `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed; `cd foundation-preview && npm run test:components -- tests/rhythm-editor.component.test.tsx tests/rhythm-history.component.test.tsx tests/app-preview.component.test.tsx` passed **118/118 tests across 3 files**. The browser test used synthetic preview storage only; live Tend data, the database and the shared 4173 origin were not accessed. No production build or full reader/legacy suites were run for this focused change. Unknown-history scoring remains unavailable until a user supplies the earlier schedule; cross-tab behavior and assistive-technology behavior beyond automated coverage were not re-evaluated.

## Daily connection sample schedule history (2026-10-25)

**Actual saved state and root cause:** on the exact `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html` origin, the initial v11 `tend2:synthetic-preview` envelope was 1,079 bytes with no saved rhythms, dated completion records, or history IDs. “Daily connection” is the built-in `sample-connect` fixture, with three source-fixture completion IDs dated Oct 17, 18 and 20, 2026; its effective records were not copied into the envelope. The editor treated its `Daily` cadence as descriptive because the fixture had no structured `schedule`. Two shared paths then lost context: the schedule-change confirmation was rendered only for rhythms that already had a structured schedule, and first-time schedule history started today because the app derived its earliest date only from persisted completion arrays, not the effective fixture-backed `completionHistory`. The prior schedule was also omitted from the draft even though the existing score selectors already interpret the exact cadence `Daily` as a daily schedule.

**Fix:** `rhythmDraftFrom` now carries forward only the exact legacy `Daily` cadence as a known daily `originalSchedule`; other descriptive cadence labels are not guessed. The editor confirms any changed structured schedule when dated history exists, showing the known prior schedule or the descriptive cadence as context. `App.saveRhythm` derives the earliest date from the active, resolved completion history, including fixture records and effective overrides. When the first structured schedule is saved for an edited rhythm with earlier dated activity, `saveRhythmDraft` starts history at the oldest known completion and records both the known prior schedule (or an honest unknown interval when none is known) and the new current schedule. Same-day changes still do not invent a prior date boundary.

**Actual preview result:** confirmed the existing `sample-connect` in the editor as Monday/Wednesday/Friday, then reloaded the same 4174 origin. Its shared history now shows **2 seasons**: **Daily**, Oct 17–24, and **Mon · Wed · Fri · Current**, Oct 25, 2026–Present. The Today card shows Mon/Wed/Fri and “Last tended Oct 20”; Sunday Oct 25 is not labeled Due today. The expanded score evidence shows 3 of 8 Daily dates completed through Oct 24, Consistency 38, On Track 60, and Health 43. After the save and reload, the envelope contains the `sample-connect` schedule overlay and those two boundaries; its dated completion array and removed-seed IDs for this rhythm remain empty, so the three fixture completion IDs/dates remain in their original source and no completion record was recreated, removed, or edited. The persisted envelope is 1,869 bytes. Port 4174 returned HTTP 200 and was left running.

**Verification and limits:** the new domain regression verifies the exact fixture-backed Daily → Monday/Wednesday/Friday transition, both schedule boundaries, and the Oct 17 tracking start. The app regression verifies confirmation content, visible/reloaded Seasons, preserved three fixture moments, no Sunday due label, and selector-derived score meters. `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed. `cd foundation-preview && npm run test:components -- tests/rhythm-editor.component.test.tsx tests/app-preview.component.test.tsx --reporter=dot` passed **71/71 tests across 2 files**. `git diff --check` passed after the code/test changes. Browser verification used only the synthetic preview key; the actual initial envelope had no saved Daily rhythm to repair, so the user-requested M/W/F schedule was saved as an overlay on the existing fixture rather than inferred from or copied into historical records. The preview intro continues to state that schedule metadata does not control completion eligibility; due-label verification was limited to the current Sunday state.

## Effective current-Season display follow-up (2026-10-25)

**Root cause and shared fix:** scoring already interpreted an exact `Daily` cadence without a structured `schedule` as `{ frequency: 'daily' }`; Seasons counted and rendered only `rhythm.schedule` or saved schedule-history segments. Sample-backed Daily connection therefore had a known schedule and three fixture completion records for scoring/history, while the Seasons view could still show zero and “Choose a schedule.” Added the pure `effectiveRhythmSchedule` helper and reused it in scoring, editor draft initialization, last-tended calculation, and `RhythmHistoryView`. The history view now counts and renders a display-only current Season from that same effective schedule when no current historical segment exists. A missing start date is omitted, and no Season/history record is created or saved. Existing schedule-history entries remain authoritative for their dated intervals; `Change schedule` protection remains independently tied to dated logs or preserved history.

**Coverage and reuse:** added a sample-backed regression using the stable `sample-connect` rhythm, checking its current Daily Season both with its three built-in sample completions and with no completion history, and checking that no start date is fabricated. Existing history tests continue to cover stored Seasons, multiple Seasons, unknown/descriptive schedules, and read-only behavior. Reuse map: `effectiveRhythmSchedule` → score selector, shared Rhythm editor, last-tended selector, and shared history view; `RhythmHistoryView` → Today, Marriage, and archived detail/history contexts through the existing detail composition. No parallel Season card or editor was added. Runtime component tests cover behavior; there is no static architecture check.

## Seasons palette and disclosure refinements (2026-10-25)

**Palette root cause and fix:** legacy schedule-history conversion created each Season through `createRhythmSeason` without an ordinal, so it chose a deterministic ID hash instead of the requested chronological palette. The screenshot-backed Daily Season was stored with `plum`; its current Season already had the correct second token `sage`. The shared history view now recognizes only a hash-generated token that differs from that Season's expected chronological token, and presents the chronological token; intentional stored tokens remain stable. V7/V8 schedule-history conversion now assigns ordinal tokens directly. Explicit Season deletion materializes any displayed legacy corrections into the saved surviving Seasons and the Undo payload, so subsequent deletion/Undo does not recolor survivors by their new array positions. Existing stable tokens and the five-color cycle remain the only palette representation; unknown-history intervals remain gray.

**Shared presentation:** added “Current Season” immediately above the current card and used the same heading class, typography, line height and spacing as “Earlier Seasons.” The expanded section has one explanation, exactly “Seasons keep track of schedule changes. Your logs stay saved and are scored using the schedule for their date.” Removed the duplicate footer explanation and “See how your schedule has changed” subtitle. The existing current badge, timeline, card, info disclosure, correction workflow and historical impact warnings remain.

**Coverage and reuse:** added domain coverage for correcting legacy hash-assigned colors, the existing seven-Season cycle coverage, and deletion/Undo coverage that starts with hash-assigned Seasons and verifies stable corrected colors after removing and restoring a boundary. V7/V8 storage migration tests verify chronological tokens. Shared component coverage verifies card/timeline palette agreement, both matching headings and the single explanation. Reuse remains `RhythmHistoryView` and its one `SeasonCard` composition across Today, Marriage and archived details; no alternate category presentation was introduced. Runtime tests cover the behavior; no static architecture check was added.

**Actual 4174 browser verification:** used the existing two-Season `sample-connect` (`Daily connection`) from the saved synthetic preview. The browser renders **Daily/earlier** in blue `#2F8CFF` and **M R F/current** in teal `#20B99A`; its timeline segments and card accents match. “Current Season” and “Earlier Seasons” both measure 13px font, 18.2px line height and 2px bottom margin. Only the requested single explanation appears; the section has no horizontal overflow. Collapse and expand were checked; selecting the earlier timeline segment focused the Daily card; opening its Edit flow showed the existing correction dialog and cancel returned without saving. Health 43, Consistency 38, On Track 60, the existing schedule/name, and all three visible sample logs remained unchanged. The synthetic envelope remained **2,509 bytes** with the same raw palette tokens (`plum` for the earlier Season and `sage` for current), no saved dated-completion overrides, and no modified names, schedules or records. ## Season proposal summary and end-date draft repair (2026-10-25)

**Root cause and fix:** the shared schedule form previously emitted only a valid schedule. When an enabled end-date field was blank or otherwise invalid, it emitted `undefined`; the Season editor consequently discarded the still-selected cadence, rendered “Choose a schedule,” and gave the score preview only a generic schedule error. The shared fields now expose a typed draft separately from the valid schedule, including the displayable cadence, end-date toggle/value, and field-specific validation. Enabling an end date initializes it from the scoring-local as-of date (or the existing schedule-field fallback); invalid end dates keep the cadence visible but block scoring/save with an end-date error. Proposed now presents cadence, time of day, starting date, and the selected end boundary from that same draft. Previous retains its known cadence and time metadata while stating “Start date not recorded” when appropriate. Weekly remains a single-choice weekday control and the existing date-boundary restrictions are unchanged.

## Weekly versus one-day Select Days and modal initial focus (2026-10-26)

**Schedule distinction and compatibility:** an explicit `weeklyMode: 'select-days'` marker distinguishes a one-day Select Days choice from Weekly while keeping the existing `frequency: 'weekly'` representation. The marker is valid only for a one-weekday weekly schedule; multi-day Select Days remains distinguishable by its weekday count. Weekly Monday accepts a completion elsewhere in the applicable calendar week; Select Days Monday accepts only a Monday completion. Both retain separate logs and count at most one credit per occurrence. The distinction flows through the shared schedule controls, current schedules, historical Season segments, correction drafts, preview/save/reload, and scoring. Schema v12 accepts the marker; schema-v11 hydration leaves ambiguous single-weekday schedules unmarked and does not infer intent, preserving their existing Weekly period-credit behavior. Hydration remains read-only; the first explicit v12 replacement preserves exact v11 source bytes under the immutable v11 recovery key before replacing the active envelope.

**Approved transition and Custom behavior:** for Monthly on the 1st through Oct 14, changing to Monthly on the 20th effective Oct 15, the Oct 17 new-Season log credits only the Oct 20 occurrence. Oct 1 remains a separate uncredited occurrence clipped to the earlier Season; the two eligible occurrences yield one Consistency credit, with no cross-Season match or duplicate credit. The overdue Oct 1 event is reset by the later qualifying Oct 17 completion, leaving zero outstanding On Track misses. Custom intervals retain exact-date matching; Custom Consistency remains unavailable because no lookback policy is approved. Descriptive cadence does not imply a structured recurrence.

**Shared reuse map and limits:** `rhythm-occurrences.ts` owns the common period/matching and due-date evidence used by `rhythm-scoring.ts`, schedule warnings, and due/completion explanations. `rhythm-scoring.ts` supplies Consistency, On Track, Health, history evidence, and both Season impact previews. `RhythmScheduleFields` is shared by Rhythm editing and Season correction; `RhythmSeasonImpact` is shared by correction and deletion; `RhythmHistoryView` is consumed by Today, Marriage, and archived details. The Season dialog continues to use `MoneyGoalDialog`; its normal initial focus now targets the optional Season name rather than a native date input, avoiding the blue-selected month segment while retaining keyboard access to the date field and existing focus restoration. Runtime/component contracts cover behavior, persistence and visible semantics; there is no static architecture checker or screen-reader evaluation.

**Browser verification and data preservation:** on port 4174, Weekly Monday with a Tuesday log in the same week displayed Health/Consistency/On Track 100/100/100. Select Days Monday with only a Tuesday log displayed 23/0/90; Select Days Monday with Monday and Tuesday logs displayed 100/100/100, with both logs still visible. Monthly due on the 1st with Oct 17, 18, and 20 logs displayed Consistency 100 with 1 completed of 1 eligible and all 3 logs retained. Quarterly due Oct 1 with an Oct 17 log and Yearly due Feb 28 with an Oct 17 log each displayed 100/100/100. The Oct 15 Monthly transition displayed 2 eligible occurrences, 1 completed, and 1 not completed; its evidence showed the Oct 1 miss, Oct 15 schedule change, and Oct 20 Tend, while history retained the Oct 17 log and both Seasons. These were isolated synthetic records and did not edit the seeded Daily connection. The exact pre-test preview envelope was restored on both `localhost:4174` and `127.0.0.1:4174`: 1,079 bytes, SHA-256 `b25b39a0105ea20d645eaaa8f141429cb94ee2414299c474f359187194a40633`, with only `tend2:synthetic-preview` in local storage. The actual Edit season dialog was opened and canceled; its name field had focus, the date month segment was not highlighted, and the same envelope fingerprint remained. Port 4174 is still running.

## Rhythm Season history coverage repair (2026-10-26)

**Root cause:** establishing the first dated Season boundary when a Rhythm had a structured current schedule but no scoring history wrote only the proposed segment, dropping the schedule that had been saved before the boundary. Separately, history parsing repaired gaps using persisted dated records but did not include resolved seeded completion history; the app could therefore show only the current Season when its older logs lived in fixture history. The count excluded explicit unknown-history intervals.

**Fix:** first-boundary creation now retains the pre-boundary saved schedule, time of day, and original effective start date; when that start is unavailable it falls back to the earliest known completion date. The selected new start remains a distinct boundary, so the previous interval ends the day before it. Existing stored schedule segments are retained. When dated logs predate stored history and there is no persisted prior schedule to recover, a single explicit unknown interval covers the missing dates; loading derives/extends that interval without writing browser storage, and explicit schedule changes persist the repaired history. Repeated repairs extend the same unknown interval rather than adding duplicates. Unknown intervals count as historical periods but do not receive invented schedule scores. Saved `timeOfDay` is also carried through Rhythm resolution so it remains available when creating the prior Season.

**Coverage:** domain tests verify the Oct 23/24 boundary, prior schedule lookup and scoring for Oct 17, 18 and 20 under the prior Daily schedule, unknown-history unavailability, fallback dates, and idempotent repair. App integration tests verify that a first boundary yields two displayed Seasons, preserves all three completion records, reloads without duplicates, and that unrecoverable history is shown as unknown on repeated read-only loads without changing storage.

**Verification (2026-10-26):** `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed. `cd foundation-preview && npm test` passed: reader **7/7**, components **375/375 across 21 files**, legacy **175/175** (**557 total**). `cd foundation-preview && npm run build` passed, with existing Vite warnings that `/tend.css` remains unresolved until runtime and that a minified chunk exceeds 500 kB. `git diff --check` passed. The real port 4174 preview was inspected read-only; it returned HTTP 200, had no open dialogs, and only `tend2:synthetic-preview` in local storage, still **1,865 bytes** with SHA-256 `9cebf086524b78b0d2065e724c1e016e7611fa7236069715fa08443db2ca81b8`. The exact new-boundary cases were verified through isolated app integration tests rather than modifying the shared browser records.

**Recovery limit:** completion records alone cannot identify which schedule was active during an unrecorded historical interval. When stored schedule history does not contain that schedule, the app shows “Earlier schedule needs details” and leaves affected historical scoring unavailable until the user supplies the missing schedule; it does not infer the current schedule retroactively. No schema, scoring policy, completion record, live account, or existing preview record was changed.

## Stable Season palette collision repair (2026-10-26)

**Root cause and behavior:** stored palette tokens are stable per Season, but legacy and boundary-creation paths can leave adjacent or nearby Seasons with the same token. The previous display compatibility rule only corrected a duplicate when it matched the Season ID hash and differed from its chronological ordinal; other valid duplicate tokens remained visually identical. `rhythmSeasonPaletteAssignments` now preserves non-colliding stored assignments and resolves collisions over a rolling window of the five approved, visually distinct palette colors. For a collision it probes the existing cycle from a deterministic Season-ID-derived starting color, selecting the first unused color. Rendering sorts by actual start date and stable ID, not current/earlier status or card order; appending a Season does not change earlier resolved assignments. After all five colors are used, the oldest becomes eligible for deterministic reuse. Unknown-history intervals stay neutral and do not consume a palette color.

**Coverage and reuse:** domain tests cover three colliding stored tokens, the `ocean`/`clay` duplicate-color mapping, deterministic results with reversed input, unchanged existing assignments when a fourth Season is appended, and the five-color reuse cycle. Component coverage verifies that three colliding Seasons receive three different visible colors and that each timeline segment and card use the same Season assignment. Deletion/Undo coverage now asserts that the resolved palette is preserved by stable Season identity rather than expecting colors based on array position. Reuse remains the existing `RhythmHistoryView` and its shared `SeasonCard`; both cards and timeline read the same assignment map. No parallel Season renderer or CSS palette was added. Runtime domain/component tests protect this contract; there is no static architecture checker, and tests do not replace assistive-technology evaluation.

**Browser and data verification:** the existing `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html` was inspected before and after reload. Its two Seasons both store `fern`; the shared UI consistently presents the earlier Season in blue `#2F8CFF` and the current Season in teal `#20B99A`, matching each card to its timeline segment. The original synthetic envelope remained **2,870 bytes**, SHA-256 `6d2de759158d5a1fc290ce12f610d9d1432246873aac3fd2ad4b4d05dc702504`; no saved records or palette tokens were rewritten.

For three-Season visual verification, a separate temporary Vite preview at `http://127.0.0.1:4175/app-preview.html` used its own origin and synthetic-only fixture. All three fixture Seasons stored `fern`; the browser rendered them as blue, coral and purple across both the timeline and cards, with cards in current/earlier order and timeline segments in chronological order. Reload retained the same three assignments. The fixture was isolated from 4174. It was not used to replace or reset the existing preview. Reversed input order and appending a fourth collision were separately verified by domain tests.

## Add Season range insertion and date editing (2026-10-26)

Added the explicit **+ Add Season** action beside the Seasons heading. It opens the existing shared Season correction dialog, with a start date, optional inclusive end date, schedule, time of day, and the requested explanation that existing Seasons adjust to fit. Date edits use the same range resolver as additions. A live impact preview lists each overlapping Season as split, shortened, or replaced and warns that affected historical scores may change before Apply.

`resolveRhythmSeasonRange` is the pure timeline operation. It overlays the selected range while retaining independently established schedule, time, notes, cadence metadata, and valid coverage outside the range. Surrounding Seasons split into preserved fragments; partial overlaps are trimmed; fully covered Seasons are replaced; ranges spanning multiple boundaries and open-ended ranges are supported. Split fragments get distinct stable identities. Editing excludes the selected original from conflict calculation and represents its vacated scheduled dates as explicit unknown history rather than silently extending the old schedule. Insertion into explicit unknown history preserves uncertainty outside the supplied range. Completion records are not moved, deleted, or rewritten; resulting schedule history drives the existing score selector without changing scoring formulas.

The complete resulting Rhythm envelope is saved through the existing `writePreview` expected-raw conflict guard. Apply reports persistence errors/conflicts through the existing UI. Undo stores a guarded prior-record snapshot, refuses to overwrite later changes, and persists the restored record through the same writer. The timeline itself survives reload and repeated loading does not add duplicate fragments; the Undo affordance is session-lifetime state and is not retained after a reload. Loading remains read-only.

**Coverage and reuse:** `RhythmHistoryView` owns the heading action and composes the one shared `RhythmSeasonCorrectionDialog`; `RhythmScheduleFields` remains shared by the general Rhythm editor and correction; `RhythmSeasonImpact` remains shared with deletion preview, with no deletion-flow redesign. The pure `rhythm-season-range.ts` resolver handles the timeline and describes overlap changes; there is no new generic editor or duplicate card. Domain tests cover leap-day inclusive split, full replacement, multi-boundary trims, future bounded/open-ended insertion, unknown history, date-edit/vacated coverage, metadata and identity preservation, active score selection, invalid ranges, and guarded Undo. Component and App tests cover Add copy/preview/Apply, preserved completion evidence, persisted timeline reload without duplicate fragments, and Undo with safe reload. Existing correction tests cover daily-to-weekdays 9/3 → 5/1 scoring evidence, period-based multiple logs earning one occurrence credit, unavailable/zero/floor states and different last qualifying dates. These are runtime contracts; there is no static architecture checker or assistive-technology evaluation.

## Season-scoped Health badges (2026-10-26)

Every shared Season card now shows the Health score badge at the upper right in place of the former Current badge. `CategoryHealthBadge` is still the badge renderer used by the Marriage score row; `RhythmSeasonCard` reuses it and the same score-tone selection, while a Season-card style applies the established 48×25px rounded badge geometry and score palette independent of the Season tint. Added pure `selectRhythmSeasonScore` in `rhythm-scoring.ts`. It narrows the existing scoring selector to one stored scheduled segment, its effective tracking start, local evaluation date, and completion records inside that Season. Historical calls cap the segment at its authoritative final local date with a derived following boundary, so weekly/monthly period credit cannot leak across a Season boundary. The existing frequency-specific Consistency window, due-date and pending-today behavior, timezone matching, On Track deductions, and 75/25 Health blend remain the only scoring implementation. Current Seasons evaluate as of today; earlier Seasons evaluate on their final inclusive date. Unknown schedules, unresolved history, unavailable records, and unsupported Custom Consistency remain unavailable; upcoming cards are neutral. ## Empty first-Season deletion and earliest-log boundary (2026-10-26)

An empty first Season can now be deleted only when a later Season remains and no dated completion record falls in its interval. Membership uses the established local-date interval `[start, next season start)`, so an off-schedule log blocks deletion and a log exactly at the next Season's start belongs to the next Season. A sole remaining Season remains undeletable and is editable instead. Deletion removes only the selected Season; surviving Season identities, names, schedules, time-of-day values, palette assignments, and every completion record remain unchanged. The successor is not extended backward. The proposed tracking boundary is shown in the impact preview and moves to the earliest remaining known scheduled Season; removed dates are not scored as scheduled misses. The resulting score selector treats that explicit boundary as authoritative while retaining earlier dated logs as evidence where applicable. The existing conflict-safe Undo restores the deleted Season and its previous tracking boundary without replacing completion records; it revalidates the current history and refuses a stale/conflicting restore.

The shared pure Season range validation and save command now also reject moving the first Season's start after the earliest dated completion for that Rhythm. The editor receives the authoritative effective dated history resolved from both sample-backed and saved records, and shows a specific inline error with the earliest local date while retaining the draft. Starts before or on that date remain available under the existing earlier-history confirmation flow. With no dated records, ordinary boundary validation remains in force; undated legacy completion state does not generate a fabricated date. Opening a view or making unrelated edits does not repair historical gaps or extend tracking.

**Coverage and reuse:** pure range tests cover first-season starts before/on/after the earliest log, including timezone-local date handling and undated/no-log cases. Delete-domain and app integration tests cover empty-first eligibility, off-schedule blocking, next-boundary ownership, sole-Season restrictions, record/identity/color preservation, tracking-boundary score behavior, save/reload, deletion preview, successful Undo, unrelated logs and conflicting history. The shared history view continues to own the Delete action and restriction text; `RhythmSeasonDeleteDialog` reuses `RhythmSeasonImpact` for the score preview, and the editor uses the same pure range command for validation and Apply. Runtime domain/component contracts protect these behaviors; there is no static architecture checker.

Added an earlier Sep 1–30, 2026 Daily Season ahead of the Oct 26 current Season; the Add preview showed the explicit earlier-tracking warning and no activity in range. Editing that earlier Season and opening Delete displayed the actual Sep 1–Oct 25 interval, the no-logs eligibility statement, the next Season's Oct 26 start, and a Before/After impact where the after scores were unavailable rather than counting the removed dates as scheduled misses. Confirming deletion left only the current Season and reported that logs were preserved; Undo restored both Seasons and the original score state. A subsequent stale Undo of the earlier Add was safely refused after the intervening delete/restore changed the history. The isolated 4176 preview was reset to its original sample state after inspection. The existing 4174 preview was not used or modified; its server remains the intended preview. No log-bearing Rhythm existed in the isolated browser fixture, so logged/off-schedule deletion blocking and earliest-log date errors were verified by automated tests rather than browser interaction.

**Root cause:** the 4174 envelope for `sample-connect` stores `scoring.trackingStartDate: 2026-08-01`, timezone `America/New_York`, and schedule-history segments V2 (Aug 1–Sep 30, Daily), Pre-Dev (Oct 1–16 interval, Daily schedule ending Oct 13), Select Days (Oct 17–20), and During Development (Oct 21 onward). There is no separate tracking-confirmation audit field, so the saved boundary is authoritative but its historical confirmation provenance cannot be independently proven from this envelope. No boundary or saved data was changed.

V2 is inside tracking. At its scheduled end Sep 30, Consistency measures Sep 1–29: 29 eligible occurrences, 0 credited; On Track has 60 overdue occurrences from Aug 1–Sep 29 and scores 0. Health is a valid 0. The prior Pre-Dev card differed because its history interval ended Oct 16 but its stored schedule ends Oct 13. `selectRhythmSeasonScore` passed Oct 16 into the shared selector, whose inactive-schedule guard returned unavailable before scoring Pre-Dev's tracked occurrences. The editor's affected schedule range already ended Oct 13 and correctly showed scoreable misses.

The pure Season selector now evaluates a historical Season at `min(Season interval end, stored schedule end, as-of date)`. This does not extend tracking or infer additional dates; it evaluates only while the saved schedule is effective. If tracking instead began Oct 17, both V2 and Pre-Dev evaluate wholly before tracking and are unavailable, as covered by tests. A Season overlapping a later tracking boundary scores only from that boundary. Existing current Season behavior remains as-of-today; its five Oct 21–25 daily misses yield Consistency 0, On Track 50, Health 12.5 (display 13). Select Days remains 100 from its three credited eligible occurrences. No log-count gate or scoring formula was added.

## Season color consistency (2026-10-27)

**Cause and correction:** `clay` and `ocean` are legacy tokens for the same coral color. When adjacent saved Seasons collided visually, the resolver started its replacement search from a Season-ID hash, so the second Season could jump to purple rather than use the next color in the established chronological cycle. Collision fallback now starts from that scheduled Season's chronological ordinal and selects the next unused palette color. Non-colliding saved tokens remain unchanged; stored Season data is not rewritten. The approved cycle remains coral (`ocean`), yellow (`sky`), green (`sage`), blue (`fern`), purple (`plum`); unknown-history intervals remain neutral gray.

**Reuse and coverage:** `rhythmSeasonPaletteAssignments` remains the single pure resolver used by Season cards, timeline segments, and the saved baseline in the correction dialog. All use the same resolved assignment; `RhythmSeasonCard` renders scheduled colors and reserves gray for `kind: 'unknown'`. Domain tests cover legacy alias collisions, chronological fallback, reversed input stability, appending Seasons, and unknown intervals not consuming palette positions. The shared-history test covers matching card/timeline assignments and unknown-history styling. No separate palette or card renderer was added.

**Actual 4174 preview:** read-only inspection of `Daily connection` found two scheduled Seasons: Oct 1 (`clay`) and Oct 17 (`ocean`). Both were stored as before; no unknown interval or gray Season was present in that actual preview. The earlier card and timeline now render coral `#FF7954`; the current card and timeline render yellow `#FFD039`. The saved tokens and date boundaries remain unchanged. The only local-storage key remained `tend2:synthetic-preview`, at 1,882 bytes with the same browser-side checksum before and after inspection; no preview records were changed. Gray remains the intentional treatment for explicit unknown history, covered by component tests but not present in this saved Rhythm.

## Correcting the active Season palette order (2026-10-27)

**Why the prior fix missed the reported cards:** the prior browser check inspected a different `4174` canvas profile containing `Daily connection` with `clay`/`ocean` tokens, not the user's active `seasons-e2e-resume` profile. That profile contains the saved Rhythm `Seasons same-day boundary reproduction` (`user-rhythm-b6dfbc84-dd52-48cb-8c7c-3d52b14f275f`). Its Oct 5 and Oct 25 scheduled Seasons both store `fern`. The prior collision resolver preserved the first blue token but chose yellow for the second because `rhythmSeasonPaletteCycleTokens` still used the stale coral/yellow/teal/blue/purple order. The exact stable IDs are `season:user-rhythm-b6dfbc84-dd52-48cb-8c7c-3d52b14f275f:2026-10-05:unknown` and `season:user-rhythm-b6dfbc84-dd52-48cb-8c7c-3d52b14f275f:2026-10-25`.

**Correction and coverage:** the shared ordinal cycle is now Blue `fern` `#2F8CFF`, Teal `sage` `#20B99A`, Yellow `sky` `#FFD039`, Coral `ocean` `#FF7954`, Purple `plum` `#A653F5`, then repeat. The collision resolver continues to preserve non-colliding saved tokens and deterministically resolves duplicate visual colors by stable chronological identity; it does not recolor every Season from its current array index. The exact saved two-`fern` history now resolves to first `fern` and second `sage`. The same assignment map continues to feed cards and timeline segments. No card/component props, score logic, layout, or saved record was changed.

Both cards and matching timeline segments were inspected in the actual preview; screenshots confirmed the teal current card and blue-to-teal timeline. ## Daily connection legacy Season color correction (2026-10-27)

**Actual affected state and cause:** the screenshot corresponds to the existing `season-editor-desktop` 4174 browser profile, not the previously inspected `seasons-e2e-resume` profile. Its `sample-connect` Rhythm is **Daily connection**. The Oct 17 interval is `kind: 'unknown'` with stable ID `season:sample-connect:2026-10-17:unknown`; its token is not used for display because unknown history is deliberately neutral. The Oct 26 current scheduled Season has stable ID `season:sample-connect:2026-10-26`, stored token `plum`, and a legacy ID-hash token of `plum`. It is the first scheduled Season because unknown intervals do not consume palette positions. The previous correction handled collisions only, so this lone hash-assigned token remained purple instead of the approved first scheduled color, blue.

**Correction:** the shared pure resolver now recognizes a saved token that still matches its stable ID-hash assignment and, only when it differs from that scheduled Season's ordinal color, resolves it to the approved chronological token. Explicit stored colors that do not match the legacy ID hash remain stable; collisions still use the ordered palette resolver. The resulting color remains derived at presentation time: cards, timeline and the saved correction baseline consume the same resolver, and no stored token is rewritten. Normal deletion persists resolved colors for surviving Seasons; Undo restores the original saved history and therefore the same derived presentation. Unknown history remains gray.

**Actual 4174 verification:** before the change, MWF Schedule's card and Oct 26 timeline segment were purple; the earlier Oct 17–25 unknown card and segment were neutral gray. After HMR and a full page reload, the current card and matching timeline segment both resolve to Blue `#2F8CFF` (`fern`); the unknown card and segment remain `#a5a9a6`. Stored tokens remain `plum` and `fern`, stable IDs/dates and schedule are unchanged. The only local-storage key remains `tend2:synthetic-preview`, **1,857 bytes**, checksum `790e09a7` before and after reload; reading/rendering did not write preview data. Port 4174 is still served by Vite from this checkout's `foundation-preview` directory.

## Seasons closeout and Foundation first-slice assessment (2026-10-27)

### Season checkpoint

The accumulated Season work in this checkout is intentional preview source, domain logic, tests, and this handoff. It covers shared Season cards and palette resolution; date-range add/edit, deletion and guarded Undo; Season-scoped score evidence; the shared editor, impact summary, keyboard action order, and responsive presentation. The additional four files are `RhythmSeasonCard.tsx`, `rhythm-season-range.ts`, `rhythm-season-range.domain.test.ts`, and `rhythm-season-scoring.domain.test.ts`. No generated output, temporary fixture, browser envelope, credential, package/dependency change, or unrelated app change is included. Port 4174 returned HTTP 200 after the production build; it was left running and its preview data was not opened for writing or modified.

Review began on branch `tend2-season-impact-clarity`, HEAD `df2a92958a4c9c95e588aca04caa2cf7903d2196`, with configured upstream `origin/main` at the same SHA. The full test run first exposed nine stale assertions for the renamed Apply action, approved palette order, and Select Days order; those assertions were updated without changing their behaviors. The fresh final checks then passed: semantic typecheck; `npm test` (reader **7/7**, component/domain **428/428 across 23 files**, legacy **175/175**, **610 total**); production build; and `git diff --check`. Vite still warns that `/tend.css` is resolved at runtime and that the minified JavaScript chunk exceeds 500 kB. No visual desktop or assistive-technology audit was performed as part of this closeout.

### Existing Foundation behavior and sources

The active legacy app loads `app-categoryrhythms.js` from `index.html`. It defines six stable Marriage Foundation IDs and stores primary/secondary Foundation references on Rhythm records. Custom Marriage Rhythms can edit these associations; the change handler updates the record and calls the existing save/render path. The Marriage Foundations tab renders a score overview and a Foundation detail with its supporting Rhythm evidence, empty state, recommendations, and rationale. Foundation cards navigate to details; Foundation badges in Rhythm details navigate back to a Foundation. In the legacy Foundation detail itself, supporting Rhythms are plain articles rather than links to shared Rhythm details/history.

The live Marriage score rule is documented in its “How Marriage scores work” disclosure and exercised by `tests/marriage-page.test.js`: active associated Rhythms contribute through frequency-weighted consistency; both primary and secondary associations contribute; one scored supporting Rhythm receives a 0.90 breadth factor; Foundations with no eligible Rhythm score remain unavailable and are excluded from the Marriage Health average. This code is loaded by the current app and is not an unused helper. It is an established Marriage rule, not evidence that the same formula applies to other categories or completable Foundations.

In the isolated React preview, `FoundationCard` in `foundation-preview/src/components/Cards.tsx` is shared by the Marriage list/detail and the catalogue’s Marriage/Finances examples. `MarriageView` already renders synthetic primary/secondary associations and composes supporting Rhythms through shared `RhythmCard`; its `onDetails` callback opens the shared `RhythmDetailsDialog`/`RhythmHistoryView`, so the sample preview supports Foundation-to-Rhythm detail/history navigation. The Marriage sample is read-only. Its Foundation and Rhythm values are fixed `illustrativeScore` fields, and the summary is static; these are not derived Foundation scores. The empty Marriage fixture reports unavailable scores and no supporting sample Rhythms. The Finances milestone in `CatalogApp` is explicitly sample-only: its completion toggle is local component state, not persisted Finance functionality. No Foundation create/edit/archive flow exists in the React preview.

The relevant project boundaries are `AGENTS.md` (no new scoring formula or finalized Finance taxonomy), `README.md` (synthetic-only preview and no live account behavior), and this handoff. The README's original “no scoring” description predates the implemented Rhythm/Season selectors; the React Foundation scores remain illustrative rather than calculated. No additional cross-category Foundation scoring specification was found.

### Recommended first implementation slice

The smallest useful missing slice is **durable Foundation association editing and shared navigation for the isolated Marriage preview**, without Foundation scoring or changes to the existing app. The stable `primaryFoundationId`/`secondaryFoundationIds` currently exist only on synthetic preview fixture objects; `RhythmEditor`, `SavedRhythm`, and strict saved-rhythm validation do not expose or persist them. If associations are editable and expected to survive reload, that concrete requirement needs a narrowly versioned addition to the synthetic envelope validator/migration; it does not justify a broad schema cleanup or any live-data migration.

Acceptance criteria: use stable Marriage Foundation IDs in both association fields; preserve primary/secondary labels and references through edit, save, reload, and Rhythm archive/restore; report unresolved references rather than silently dropping them; reuse `FoundationCard`, `RhythmCard`, `RhythmDetailsDialog`, and `RhythmHistoryView`; and ensure opening a supporting Rhythm and returning to its Foundation preserves accessible focus/navigation. Keep ongoing Marriage Foundations non-completable and keep completable milestones capability-gated; do not introduce Finance Foundations or inferred taxonomy. Keep all Foundation values explicitly illustrative/unavailable in this slice.

Reuse map: `FoundationCard` → Marriage overview/detail and component catalogue; `RhythmCard` → Marriage supporting-Rhythm list; `RhythmDetailsDialog` → selected Rhythm details; `RhythmHistoryView` → the shared detail/history surface. Existing React views already share these components; no parallel Foundation card or Rhythm-detail implementation is recommended. The legacy HTML renderer remains a separate existing app surface and is out of this preview implementation. Runtime component/domain tests can protect association round-tripping and navigation contracts; there is no static architecture checker, and automated tests do not replace assistive-technology review.

### Product decisions required before Foundation scoring

- Whether the existing active Marriage rule (frequency-weighted primary/secondary supporting Rhythm consistency, 0.90 breadth factor for one scored Rhythm, and average of scored Foundations) applies only to Marriage or is intended to generalize. The current implementation and disclosure settle Marriage; no cross-category rule is documented.
- Whether unscored supporting Rhythms are omitted from a Foundation aggregate and whether an empty/no-eligible Foundation remains unavailable. The active Marriage behavior already answers this for Marriage: unavailable, excluded from its aggregate, never coerced to zero.
- Whether ongoing and completable Foundations differ in score eligibility or aggregation. Current capability metadata and UI settle only lifecycle actions (ongoing has no completion action; a milestone may be completed); they do not specify score treatment.

No Foundation UI or scoring was implemented in this closeout.

### Next task: agree Foundation scoring rules before implementation

Foundation scoring remains unimplemented in the preview. Foundations are author-defined presets, not user-created in the initial scope. Unused Foundations and Foundations without scorable supporting Rhythms must remain unavailable (`—`), not be assigned zero. Existing Marriage scoring rules remain documented separately and are not generalized by this association slice.

## Foundation associations as unweighted links (2026-10-27)

**Approved behavior:** a Rhythm is either linked to a Foundation or not. A Rhythm may link to multiple Foundations, and linked Rhythms are intended to have equal weight within a Foundation. This settles the association semantics only; Foundation score calculation remains unimplemented.

**Implementation:** the Foundation association editor now uses labeled checkboxes and the concise instruction “Choose the rhythms that support this Foundation.” Current selections track the draft; the supporting-Rhythm list and its accessibility region use “Linked Rhythms” without exposing Primary/Supporting distinctions. IDs remain internal stable keys and are not rendered as ordinary UI text; cadence/time metadata distinguishes duplicate titles. Save/Cancel, retained save errors, keyboard interaction, archived read-only behavior, and shared Rhythm details/history navigation remain intact.

**Compatibility and data safety:** no storage migration or new relationship store was added. Existing Rhythm-owned `primaryFoundationId` and `secondaryFoundationIds` are interpreted as one ordered union of unique Foundation IDs. Existing primary remains the compatibility primary while linked; if removed, the first surviving secondary in existing order is promoted, and remaining links are preserved. Duplicate links are checked and displayed once; when a user changes a Rhythm's link set and explicitly saves, that Rhythm is written as a unique canonical set. Unchanged links are left byte-for-byte in their existing internal representation. Merely hydrating state or opening/cancelling the editor does not write or normalize the envelope. Foundation/Rhythm entities and unrelated associations are not deleted.

## Equal-weight Marriage Foundation scoring (2026-10-27)

**Formula and eligibility:** `selectFoundationScore` reads the existing Rhythm-owned Foundation associations as an ordered unique set of stable Rhythm IDs. Archived links are excluded. A Rhythm is eligible only when the existing `selectRhythmScore` result has numeric Consistency and On Track metrics; the exact same eligible set feeds both arithmetic means. Foundation Health is 75% of the unrounded Consistency mean plus 25% of the unrounded On Track mean. No frequency weighting, breadth penalty, Primary/Supporting distinction, or repeated contribution within a Foundation is applied. A shared Rhythm may contribute once to each linked Foundation. Numeric zero remains a score; no eligible Rhythm (including an empty Foundation) yields unavailable values (`—`). Excluded score results, their reasons, unresolved score-result IDs, linked IDs and eligible IDs remain in selector evidence.

**Shared state and presentation:** `selectFoundationRhythmScores` builds one map of existing Rhythm score-selector results from the effective Marriage Rhythms, saved overrides, merged completion history, schedule/Season metadata, archive state, and one shared evaluation instant. It uses each Rhythm's established scoring timezone and local-date conversion. The map is derived on the normal App render path; there are no per-card timers or persisted score fields. Association, completion, schedule, Season and archive/restore state changes therefore produce a fresh result through the existing state flow. Both Marriage Foundation overview and detail compose `FoundationCard`, show derived Health, and expose partial eligibility such as “1 of 4 linked Rhythms scored”; the detail also reports archived/unresolved/excluded links. The shared Info disclosure explains equal contribution and the 75/25 blend. The Marriage summary/category totals remain explicitly illustrative and untouched; Rhythm scoring and broader aggregations were not changed.

**Reuse map and duplication review:** `selectRhythmScore` → `selectFoundationRhythmScores` → `selectFoundationScore`; shared `FoundationCard` → Marriage Foundation overview and detail; shared `RhythmCard` → Marriage supporting-Rhythm list; shared `RhythmDetailsDialog`/`RhythmHistoryView` → supporting-Rhythm details and history. No category-specific scoring/card copy, new persistence model, schema migration, or parallel history UI was added. The small Foundation selector is separate because it resolves linked IDs, applies Foundation eligibility and produces per-Foundation averages while leaving Rhythm selector behavior unchanged. Runtime domain/component tests cover the contracts; there is no static architecture checker, and the tests do not replace assistive-technology review.
