## Calculated Marriage Category summary (2026-10-27)

**Approved formula and eligibility:** the Marriage summary now consumes the already-derived Foundation results; it does not average Rhythms directly or recalculate Foundation scores. Unique eligible Foundations must have numeric Consistency and On Track results, and that same set feeds both arithmetic means. Marriage Health is 75% of the unrounded Consistency mean plus 25% of the unrounded On Track mean. Every eligible Foundation contributes equally; unavailable Foundations are excluded rather than treated as zero, while a genuine numeric zero remains eligible. Duplicate Foundation IDs are counted once. With no eligible Foundations, Health, Consistency and On Track are all unavailable (`—`). Values are rounded only in visible score text; shared meters receive the unrounded results. A Rhythm shared across Foundations contributes through each Foundation result once, then each eligible Foundation contributes once to Marriage.

**Shared state and presentation:** the new pure typed `selectCategoryScore` selector consumes the existing Foundation selector results. `MarriageView` computes the Foundation results once and passes them both to the existing Foundation presentation and Marriage summary, so association, history/log, schedule, Season and archive state updates continue through the existing App state/evaluation refresh flow. The summary replaces the illustrative Marriage health and Rhythms/Foundations score pair with calculated Health, Consistency, On Track, unique eligible Foundation coverage and a concise equal-weight information reveal. Existing shared score meters and the Marriage summary layout are reused. The overall-life summary and other category summaries remain illustrative and explicitly labelled as such; no Foundation, Rhythm, schema, persistence or legacy-app scoring behavior changed.

**Reuse map and duplication review:** `selectRhythmScore` → `selectFoundationScore` → `selectCategoryScore`; `FoundationCard` remains shared by Marriage Foundation overview and detail, while the Marriage summary composes the existing shared score-meter presentation. No new Foundation/Rhythm score calculation, parallel card, or duplicated score meter was introduced. The new selector is separate because Category eligibility/deduplication and equal-weight aggregation are a distinct pure domain boundary. Domain and component/integration tests cover the selector contracts and summary refresh behavior; there is no static architecture checker, and automated tests do not replace visual or assistive-technology review.

**Verification:** semantic typecheck passed. Focused tests passed **115/115 across 4 files**. Full `cd foundation-preview && npm test` passed reader **7/7**, component/domain **467/467 across 28 files**, and legacy **175/175** (**649 total**). `cd foundation-preview && npm run build` passed; the build retained the existing runtime `/tend.css` resolution notice and main-bundle size warning (**538.34 kB** minified JavaScript). Final `git diff --check` passed.

## Cardio synthetic preview slice (2026-10-28)

**Behavior and model:** Cardio is stored separately from Strength and Weight. Its pure selectors use an explicit evaluation date and the app’s local calendar context; weeks run Monday–Sunday, and runs count by activity date. Valid run records require stable IDs, positive finite distances, valid dates no later than today, and may share a date. Invalid, duplicate-ID, unresolved, or future imported records remain in raw data with reported issues and are excluded from validated totals/history/day-strip evidence. Edits retain record IDs; deleting or changing a run affects only that record and the corresponding date/weekly totals. Run target must be a positive integer and mileage target positive and finite. Both are required for a numeric score; zero remains a valid numeric result, while missing/invalid goals are unavailable. Run and mileage percentages are individually capped at 100%; recorded totals are not capped. Cardio progress is the lower of those percentages, rounded by the existing final rounding rule. This is intentionally not the ordinary Rhythm 75/25 formula and does not change any Fitness/Foundation/category aggregate.

**Rhythm completion:** Specialized Rhythms use the shared explicit `healthLogCapability: 'cardio'` identity, not title or category inference. The shared effective schedule/Season occurrence rules determine eligibility for the selected activity date, including valid backdated dates. A run can own one optional occurrence completion. Add/edit/date-change/unlink/delete update only that run’s owned credit, preserve notes and unrelated completions, reject conflicts instead of replacing activity, and prevent duplicate or repeated-submit credit. Opening/rendering a dialog does not complete an occurrence. These are isolated synthetic-preview flows; no live data is read or migrated.

**Storage and compatibility:** the active preview-envelope schema is **v18**. Supported preceding envelopes receive an empty Cardio default, with no invented goals or runs. Before replacement, exact v17 source bytes are retained under the versioned recovery key and exposed through the existing recovery read/download path; recovery conflict or persistence failure blocks replacement. Hydration remains read-only. The existing explicit save, validated reload, and backup import/export flows carry Cardio while preserving unknown fields and existing records. No schema/database or live-account state was accessed.

**Reuse map and duplication review:** `HealthFitnessView` composes the existing Strength, Cardio, and Weight sections in Health & Fitness navigation. `CardioView` reuses shared modal framing, fields, buttons, validation, focus behavior, complete Rhythm cards, editor, details/history, and association/completion flows. The weekly measures, Monday–Sunday strip, date-only run history/form, and progress display remain in the specialized Cardio view because they are Cardio-specific; the specialized run/goals presentation does not duplicate the shared Rhythm card/editor/modal. No generic cross-activity abstraction was added. Runtime component/domain/app tests exercise these contracts; there is no static architecture checker, and the tests do not replace screen-reader or broader visual-regression review.

## Category–Foundation ownership safeguard (2026-10-28)

**Ownership rule:** exported `foundationCatalogue` is the authoritative set of current Foundations. `foundationCategoryId` resolves each catalogue Category label through the stable `areas` IDs; no Category/Foundation ID or name is hardcoded into removal logic. `foundationOwnershipError` is shared by pure Rhythm-draft validation and `saveFoundationRhythmAssociations`, so changed link sets cannot persist known Foundations without their owning Category. The catalogue-driven rule also covers the existing Finance example and is exercised with a separate synthetic Foundation fixture; no Finance taxonomy or Foundation definition was added.

**Removal and repair behavior:** the shared `RhythmEditor` stages Category IDs and Foundation IDs together while the Category picker is open. Removing a Category with owned links switches the existing picker surface to an in-place confirmation that names the Category and affected Foundations. Remove deletes only known links owned by that stable Category ID, deduplicating through `foundationRhythmIds`; Cancel returns to the staged picker unchanged. Done applies both staged arrays only to the editor draft; picker Cancel/Escape discards them, and editor Cancel discards the full draft. Re-adding a Category does not restore unlinked Foundations. Known orphan links are identified in the editor and can be explicitly removed in the Foundation picker; unresolved ID references are retained on load, diagnosed by validation, and have an explicit remove action in the editor. `validateSavedRhythmsV13` accepts nonempty unresolved IDs so they can be reviewed and repaired rather than silently discarded or blocking access to the editor; malformed/empty and duplicate references remain invalid. Hydration remains read-only. Marriage example Rhythms now carry their already-implied `marriage` Category as explicit stable IDs so direct Foundation-link changes satisfy the same rule.

**Reuse map and duplication review:** `RhythmEditor` remains the single Add/Edit/suggestion-review form and composes the existing `BadgeSelectionPicker` for Categories, People, and Foundations; the confirmation replaces picker content inside the existing `MoneyGoalDialog`, without stacking dialogs. Category/Foundations continue to use shared `AreaIcon`/`MarriageIcon`, and People keeps the existing picker groups. `foundation-rhythm-associations.ts` supplies pure ownership/removal helpers to the editor and the direct Foundation-link command. No copied editor, category-specific association flow, schema, global cleanup, or load-time write was added. Runtime/domain/component contracts cover the behavior; no static architecture checker exists, and these checks do not replace assistive-technology review. The current Finance example has no dedicated approved Foundation SVG; this change adds no icon/taxonomy design.

Focused ownership/editor tests passed **63/63 across 3 files** before the final unresolved-reference case; the final full component suite includes that additional case. `git diff --check` passed after this handoff entry.

**Browser verification and limits:** the existing `http://127.0.0.1:4174/app-preview.html` returned HTTP 200 and remains running on Today. ## Rhythm Frequency initialization and modal stability (2026-10-28)

**Root cause and behavior:** the editor could format a legacy `Daily` cadence as a known Frequency label while leaving `draft.schedule` empty. Opening schedule controls then rendered “Choose frequency,” discarding the effective value from view. `rhythmDraftFrom` now initializes from the active scheduled Season when present, otherwise from the supported effective schedule fallback; an unknown active Season remains unresolved, and descriptive cadence never implies weekdays or dates. The same current Season supplies time of day. An unchanged cadence-derived fallback is not materialized as a new saved schedule or rewritten cadence. Protected schedule cancellation restores only the opening schedule-related fields, leaving unrelated draft edits intact.

**Modal and reuse:** the shared `RhythmEditor` keeps the compact protected Frequency button for supported schedules with dated logs or preserved Season history, and direct `RhythmScheduleFields` controls for eligible records. Opening the protected flow now seeds the fields from the effective schedule. Its editor-only dialog class pins the top position and bounds height to the viewport while retaining internal scrolling; the active Frequency control is focused and revealed only as needed. Cancel restores the prior schedule and opener focus. The existing nested Season confirmation and shared schedule controls remain the only implementation; other dialogs, recurrence/scoring rules and persistence format are unchanged.

## Faith Category and Practices (2026-10-28)

No serialized schema shape or persistence version changed.

## Reusable Rhythm suggestions

Suggestion templates and saved Rhythms now have separate identities. A source is a stable Category-qualified ID such as `marriage.daily-connection`; each saved Rhythm receives its own `user-rhythm-<UUID>` ID. Source matching uses only the explicit source field, never titles or descriptions, so renamed and same-titled records remain independent. Suggestion counts include unique active instances for that source only; archived instances appear separately and use the existing restore command.

The shared picker keeps Add available, switches to an in-place instance choice for existing matches, and offers Edit, Restore, Add another, and Back without stacking dialogs. Editing keeps the Rhythm ID, source, schedule, links, history, and Seasons; fresh additions start with no copied people or history. Successful saves return to the refreshed choice view. Marriage remains the only configured catalogue; this slice adds no new suggestions or Category catalogues. The optional Category catalogue configuration and picker presentation are shared rather than copied per Category.

Schema v14 strictly validates and serializes optional `suggestionSourceId`. Pre-v14 source recovery is restricted to exact known Marriage deterministic Rhythm IDs; no source is inferred from visible content. Hydration stays read-only. Before the first explicit v14 replacement, the exact prior envelope is preserved under its immutable versioned recovery key; conflict or preservation failure blocks replacement. Existing supported backup-import compatibility remains in place. Independent Rhythm IDs continue to key schedule, archive state, completion history, Seasons, and Undo; scoring still treats eligible active instances as separate commitments.

## Strength preview modernization (2026-10-29)

**Behavior inventory traced from the legacy implementation:** Strength tracks nine muscle groups, each with a training interval and preferred rest days. Training dates come from workout history; the displayed muscle score is a training-timeliness measure, with ten points deducted per overdue day, floored at zero. Never-trained groups remain eligible and receive recommendation need 100. Missing settings use established defaults; malformed or unavailable history is distinct from never-trained history, and future-dated workouts are ignored. Rest eligibility uses calendar-day boundaries, including zero rest days for Abs. Saved-workout recommendations prioritize never-trained groups, average normalized need across each workout's unique targets, exclude a workout if any target is resting or has invalid history, order ties by stable name/ID, suppress duplicate target combinations, and return no more than two. Recommended, specific-workout, and custom-workout Rhythm modes remain distinct. Manual workout logging remains available while targeted muscles are resting. Training intervals and rest preferences retain their range and cross-field validation. Template edits do not rewrite historical activity; template deletion does not delete logs.

**Implementation and state boundaries:** `domain/strength.ts` contains the typed records, validation, explicit evaluation-date selectors, recommendation logic and create/edit/delete commands. The selectors are pure and do not read storage or clocks. The shared synthetic preview envelope is schema v15; older previews hydrate with default Strength state in memory, without load-time writes. The exact v14 source is preserved before explicit replacement with v15, and prior backups remain supported by the import path. Existing saved Rhythm, log, completion, Season and association IDs remain independent; a linked workout log owns one dated Rhythm completion and edits/deletes affect only that linked occurrence. Workout-template changes preserve the activity snapshot. No live data, app-shell files, legacy implementation, dependency, scoring formula or non-Strength Category was changed.

**Scoring language and intentional differences:** the existing training-timeliness score was preserved and is labeled as training status, not ordinary Rhythm Consistency or Foundation Health. Recommendations remain based on the configured training plan and are not medical recovery advice. No intentional recommendation or scoring behavior change was introduced.

**Presentation and reuse map:**

| Shared component or surface | Consumers |
|---|---|
| `App` Health route and synthetic preview persistence | Strength view and shared Rhythm completion flow |
| `RhythmCard` and shared Rhythm details/history | Today, Category views and Strength Rhythms |
| `PersonBadge` | Shared Rhythm associations, including Strength cards |
| `MoneyGoalDialog` modal primitive | Existing goal flow and Strength training-goal settings |
| Specialized `StrengthView` | Strength-only muscle map, selected-muscle details, training settings, saved-workout recommendations and workout logging |

The specialized map and workout template/logging forms remain separate from the shared Rhythm card and editor because they model training-specific data and interaction. No Strength-specific copy of the full Rhythm card or shared completion/history presentation was added. Domain/component/integration tests protect the typed selectors, visible and accessible map/dialog behavior, and linked-completion contract; there is no static architecture checker, so tests cannot prove the absence of all future duplicated source or replace assistive-technology review.

Final `cd foundation-preview && npm test` passed: reader **7/7**, component/domain **577/577 across 34 files**, legacy **175/175** (**759 total**). The production build passed; Vite reported the existing `/tend.css` runtime-resolution warning and a main-bundle size warning (**636.52 kB**). Final `git diff --check` passed.

## Shared minute shortcuts completed (2026-10-29)

Added **00**, **15**, **30**, and **45** shortcut buttons under the Minute column in the shared legacy `app-timewheel.js` picker. A shortcut selects the exact minute and updates the same draft state and centered wheel position as other picker selections; the hour and AM/PM remain unchanged, including midnight and noon. The existing wheel still exposes 00–59. Buttons announce their action, expose `aria-pressed` selection state, retain visible keyboard focus, and use restrained selected styling with 44 px minimum touch targets. A narrow-container CSS rule stacks the shortcut controls when two columns would be too compressed. Reduced-motion mode uses non-smooth positioning. The cache-busted `index.html` script URL was updated.

Added optional typed shortcut values to `TimeDial` and enabled them only on the Minute dial in `RhythmCompletionForm`. These buttons use the same explicit draft callback, preserve the current hour and period, and remain within the existing completion Save/Cancel flow. This preserves the separate legacy and React preview renderers rather than forcing one renderer across the existing application boundary.

## Responsive shared time picker (2026-10-29)

The React Today completion form now chooses its time-picker layout from the measured `.rhythm-completion-form__dials` width, not the device or viewport: **401 px and wider** uses Hour, Minute, and AM/PM wheels; **400 px and narrower** uses equal Hour and Minute columns with an AM/PM segmented toggle under Hour. The Minute wheel retains all 00–59 values and the 00/15/30/45 shortcuts. Hour, Minute, and AM/PM use the same compact rounded label chip with theme-token surface and text colors. In narrow mode, the centered AM/PM label is hidden from the accessibility tree because the button group already has the accessible name “AM / PM.” The AM/PM label and buttons stay beneath Hour; Minute shortcuts sit directly under the Minute wheel with only the grid's 10 px row gap, independently of the AM/PM label. Only the active AM/PM control is rendered. The same draft state survives either transition; focus moves to the equivalent selected AM/PM control when a layout change replaces the focused control.

## Weight React preview slice (2026-10-29)

Weight is now available as a separate view under Health & Fitness, beside the existing Strength view. The slice preserves starting and target weights, dates, lb/kg selection, date-only weigh-ins, notes, history, edit/delete, and the optional link to a scheduled Weight Rhythm. Empty goals remain valid; a supplied goal must be complete and valid. Measurements must be positive, finite, valid-date, and not future-dated; multiple entries on a date are retained. The latest valid measurement is selected by date then stable ID. Linear goal-path interpolation is clamped to the goal interval and supports loss, gain, maintenance, start-date, incomplete-goal, no-measurement, and expired-goal states. The existing score remains 100 minus five points per day behind, clamped to 0–100, and remains unavailable in the established unavailable cases. Weight is not added to Foundation, category, or overall aggregation, and ordinary Rhythm scoring is unchanged.

**Unit and evidence handling:** Weight goals and measurements are stored canonically in kilograms. Input and display values convert using exactly `1 lb = 0.45359237 kg`; conversion precision is retained internally and rounding is for display only. Repeated lb/kg switching therefore does not alter historical physical values. The estimate and chart use the same validated measurement evidence; malformed, unresolved, non-positive, or future-dated records remain in raw data and are reported, but are excluded from calculations and observed marks. The latest record date is prominent. When it is stale, copy says the estimate is based on that dated measurement and is not today’s measured weight. The specialized SVG chart distinguishes recorded weigh-in dots, the planned goal path, and the expected-today marker, with units, dates, and a concise legend; it does not synthesize intermediate observations or extend recorded data as current.

**Weight Rhythm ownership:** an explicit `healthLogCapability: 'weight'` on a stable Rhythm identity marks compatibility; title and category alone do not. Eligibility is checked against the schedule/Season effective on the selected log date, including valid backdated occurrences. A weigh-in may own one occurrence completion. Add/edit/date-change/unlink/delete updates only the matching completion ID owned by that weigh-in, preserves its completion note where ownership remains, and leaves unrelated activity untouched. Duplicate-credit and conflicting-ownership cases are reported instead of overwritten. No completion is created by rendering or opening a dialog; the pure `saveWeightDataCommand` is invoked only on explicit save.

**Storage compatibility:** the active synthetic preview envelope is schema v17. Earlier supported schemas receive an empty Weight default and no fabricated goals, records, or links. The first replacement from schema v16 first stores its exact original bytes under the immutable v16 recovery key; the existing recovery UI can read and download those bytes. Hydration stays read-only, replacement is blocked on recovery conflict or persistence failure, and Weight participates in validated reload and backup import/export. Only the versioned synthetic preview envelope is involved; no live account/database state is read or written.

**Reuse map and resolved duplication:** `HealthFitnessView` composes `StrengthView` and `WeightView`; `WeightView` uses the existing `MoneyGoalDialog` modal frame, `RhythmCard` for Weight Rhythms, and `ScoreMeter`. App-level Rhythm editor, details/history, and association flows are reused through callbacks, while a typed Weight capability gates association choices. Weight’s goal and date-only measurement forms remain small specialized forms because their validation and fields differ from Strength and money goals; they share modal framing and the established field/action styles rather than duplicating a general-purpose editor. The Weight chart is a justified specialized component because recorded values, a planned path, and a separate expected marker have different semantics; the money-goal chart was not altered. Review found no copied Rhythm card/editor/history implementation. Architecture tests exercise runtime and domain contracts; they do not prove source-level reuse or replace manual assistive-technology testing.

**Verification:** `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed. The focused Weight/domain, component, App integration, and storage/migration run passed **151/151 tests in 4 files**. Final `cd foundation-preview && npm test` passed: reader **7/7**, component/domain **626/626 across 37 files**, and legacy **175/175** (**808 total**). Migration fixtures now model the actual v15 and future-v18 payload shapes after the v17 schema addition. `cd foundation-preview && npm run build` passed. Existing build notices remain: `/tend.css` resolves at runtime, and the minified main chunk is **684.05 kB**, above Vite’s 500 kB advisory threshold. `git diff --check` passed.

**Browser verification and limits:** the actual React preview was exercised at isolated synthetic origin `http://127.0.0.1:4175/app-preview.html`, not the user’s port 4174. Synthetic goals and weigh-ins were created, edited, and deleted; lb/kg switching retained canonical stored values; an explicitly weight-enabled Rhythm created a linked occurrence visible in shared history; the app was reloaded and a synthetic backup imported. At a 252 px viewport and in a rendered 1100 px iframe, the Weight view had no horizontal overflow; the wide view showed separate recorded marks and the narrow view retained chart labels and controls. The user’s port-4174 records were not read or changed. Browser checks do not replace physical-device, screen-reader, or medical-domain review. ## Health & Fitness acceptance review and checkpoint (2026-10-29)

**Acceptance review:** Strength, Weight, and Cardio are composed under the existing Health & Fitness navigation and remain separate typed data models in synthetic preview schema **v18**. The domain, component, App integration, migration/recovery, Rhythm, and legacy suites cover their established calculations, validation, history/edit/delete, explicit saves, keyboard tab behavior, dialog Escape/Cancel/focus return, and linked-completion ownership. The full suite also covers existing ordinary Rhythm, Foundation, and Category score behavior. No Health & Fitness aggregate was added; Weight and Cardio scores remain feature-local and Strength training status remains distinct from Rhythm Health.

The shared App flow uses the common `RhythmEditor`, details/history, completion records, stable Rhythm identities and explicit capabilities. Strength, Weight, and Cardio use the same shared `RhythmCard` and shared modal framing; Strength’s saved-workout People picker reuses the People association row/picker and keeps the parent draft mounted. Weight and Cardio resolve linked occurrence eligibility through the effective schedule/Season for the selected activity date and only mutate completion credit owned by that measurement/run. Conflicting or duplicate credit is rejected. Weight remains canonical kilograms with exact `1 lb = 0.45359237 kg` conversion; Cardio remains a separate Monday–Sunday, lower-of-capped-percentages measure. Strength template edits retain source identity and do not rewrite historical workouts. The time picker keeps one shared draft flow in each existing renderer: React `TimeDial`/`RhythmCompletionForm` and the separate legacy picker; minute shortcuts preserve hour/period and exact values.

**Confirmed defects corrected during acceptance:** (1) Weight’s converted values are displayed to hundredths, but the number fields required tenths; changing kg to lb could leave a saved-goal or weigh-in dialog open with native step validation silently blocking Save. All three Weight number inputs now accept hundredths (`step="0.01"`), and a component regression confirms unchanged canonical goals and measurements save after a unit switch. (2) At the 252 px narrow browser canvas, Strength Saved Workouts’ Browse/Add controls and template actions exceeded their card width. A scoped ≤380 px rule wraps those controls; the actual narrow page now has no horizontal document overflow. No scoring, template, or logging behavior changed.

**Reuse map and justified specialized surfaces:**

| Shared component | Consuming views |
|---|---|
| `HealthFitnessView` | Health & Fitness Strength, Cardio, and Weight navigation/panels |
| `RhythmCard`, `RhythmEditor`, `RhythmDetailsDialog` / `RhythmHistoryView` | shared fitness Rhythm lists, editor, details/history and completion association |
| `MoneyGoalDialog`, common form controls and action styles | Strength settings/templates/logging; Weight goals/weigh-ins; Cardio goals/runs |
| `PeopleAssociationControls` / `PersonBadge` | Rhythm associations and Strength saved-workout People |
| `TimeDial` / `RhythmCompletionForm` | React completion picker and shared draft/save flow |

`StrengthView` retains its muscle map, training-specific recommendations and workout history; Weight retains its domain-specific recorded/goal-path/expected-marker SVG chart and date-only weigh-in form; Cardio retains its weekly measures, Monday–Sunday strip and date-only run history. These are domain-specific presentations, not copied Rhythm cards or editors. Legacy `app-timewheel.js` remains a separate existing-app renderer boundary. Runtime behavior tests protect contracts but are not a static architecture checker; no screen-reader or assistive-technology audit was performed.

## Strength workout logging modal refinement (2026-10-29)

**Confirmed root cause:** the Strength logging form disabled every muscle checkbox whenever a saved workout was selected (and also for a specific-workout Rhythm). It initialized checkboxes only from an existing log, not the selected template, while save copied the template's full muscle list instead of the form selection. The label already wrapped each native checkbox, so the reported selection failure was not caused by an overlay or missing label target; disabled state and save behavior were the causes.

**Behavior and reuse:** `StrengthMuscleSelection` is the one typed, controlled, native-checkbox tile component used by Strength workout logging. Contextual saved-workout and recommended-workout entry shows “Log workout” with the template name directly below, omits the template selector, and initializes an editable selection from that template. General/custom entry retains the saved-workout/custom selector; changing templates initializes that template's muscle selection, and subsequent checkbox changes remain in component state through unrelated field updates. Saved-template editing retains its existing UI. Save stores the selected muscle snapshot and template reference on the log without changing the saved template or other logs. Resting muscles remain enabled for manual logging. Existing date/notes validation, Rhythm mode handling, linked completion ownership and cancellation are retained.

**Reuse map and duplication review:** `StrengthMuscleSelection` → saved-workout contextual logs, recommended logs, general/custom logs, linked Strength Rhythm logs, and workout-history edits, all composed by `StrengthView`. The existing saved-workout editor's checkbox list remains separate because it edits the template itself and is intentionally outside this logging control refinement. No duplicate logging form, scoring logic, or category-specific variant was added. Component tests exercise the runtime contract; there is no static architecture checker, and no screen-reader audit was performed.

**Verification:** added coverage for checkbox and whole-tile selection, Space-key selection, Upper Body initialization, contextual versus general entry, template switching, persistence of adjustments during notes entry, currently resting muscles, selected-muscle snapshots, preserving templates and unrelated history on edit, failed save, Cancel/focus return, and linked Rhythm occurrence IDs. `cd foundation-preview && npm run typecheck` passed. `cd foundation-preview && npm run test:components -- tests/strength.component.test.tsx tests/app-preview.component.test.tsx` passed **110/110 tests across 2 files**. `cd foundation-preview && npm run build` passed; existing notices were `/tend.css` retained for runtime resolution and the minified 709.92 kB JavaScript chunk exceeding Vite's 500 kB advisory threshold. `git diff --check` passed.

**Browser evidence and limits:** reproduced the defect in an isolated synthetic preview on port 4188: Upper Body opened with all nine checkboxes unchecked and disabled. After the fix, the saved-workout entry opened with Chest, Back, Biceps, Triceps and Shoulders selected, all nine checkboxes enabled, no selector, and the workout name beneath the heading. At the available 455 px viewport, the two-column tiles had 44 px minimum targets and the dialog had no horizontal overflow. The general entry retained its selector and initialized selections when a template was selected. A synthetic log with an extra selected muscle saved and remained in history after reload, while the template stayed at its original five muscles. The native dialog `requestClose()`/cancel path closed the modal and restored focus to its opener; the Canvas did not provide a physical keypress action, so this was not a manual Escape keypress. The Canvas could not be resized from its 455 px viewport, so a separate wide-browser layout check was unavailable. The synthetic preview was reset to its examples afterward and the task-owned port-4188 server was stopped. Port 4174 returned HTTP 200 and was left running; its browser data was not accessed or changed.

## Friendships first synthetic-preview slice (2026-10-29)

**Behavior and ownership:** Added category tabs for Today, Friends, and Rhythms. Friends are selected from existing synthetic People identities; the feature does not create a second People directory or activate decorative fixture friends. Today distinguishes the no-friends, no-cadence, due, and “You’re caught up.” states. Each friend has one optional Daily, Weekly, Every two weeks, or Monthly elapsed cadence; No cadence removes that setting. The next due date is inclusive: due when the current local calendar date is on or after the anchor plus the interval. Until the first connection, the explicit cadence-set date is the anchor; afterward the latest qualifying participant event date is the anchor. Monthly arithmetic clamps at month end. Changing cadence recalculates status without rewriting event history.

Each log is one stable-ID connection event with Text, Call, or Time Together type, a local date, optional local time and notes, and explicitly selected actual participants. Person histories are projections over that one event; nonparticipants remain unaffected. History displays actual participants and editable event details, and deletion removes the event once after confirmation. Clearing optional time or notes during edit removes those properties rather than retaining stale values. Connection events do not complete Rhythms, and tending a Rhythm does not create a connection. Friendships Rhythms use the existing shared Rhythm category/editor/card/completion/archive/restore paths; no suggested catalog, Foundation, scoring, or overall-life aggregation was added.

**Data and recovery:** The sole new persisted state is `friendships` within synthetic preview schema **v19**: friend IDs referencing existing synthetic People, cadence settings keyed by person ID, and a single connection-event array. Today status, histories, ordering, and month/day arithmetic remain derived; no duplicate history or derived-state writes were added. Schema-v18 envelopes migrate in memory to empty Friendships without fabricating events; hydration remains read-only. Before the first explicit v19 replacement of a v18 envelope, exact source bytes are preserved under the immutable `tend2:synthetic-preview:v18-recovery` key, exposed by the existing recovery UI, and recovery conflict/failure blocks replacement. Backup validation, export/import, corruption safeguards, and stale-write checks continue through the established preview envelope. No live account, browser origin, database, or legacy writer was used.

**Reuse map and duplication review:** `FriendshipsView` composes `AreaIcon`, `PersonBadge`/`PersonBadgeVisual`, `BadgeSelectionPicker`, `MoneyGoalDialog`, `RhythmCard`, and `ArchivedRhythmsDialog`. App category navigation supplies the existing person destination and shared Rhythm add/edit/tend/archive/restore commands. `friendships.ts` is the pure typed owner of cadence and connection selectors/commands. A small local `ConnectionEditor` exists only for connection-specific type/date/time/actual-participant/notes fields; it does not duplicate the general Rhythm editor or person model. Component tests exercise its accessible tab, save/cancel/Escape, edit/delete, history, due-state, and shared-Rhythm contracts. No static architecture checker or manual assistive-technology audit was added.

No dependency or lockfile changes were made.

Added Morgan through the existing-person picker, set Weekly cadence, confirmed the no-cadence/caught-up distinction, logged one Call with Morgan and Avery as actual participants, viewed notes/history, edited the connection, confirmed due status recomputed, deleted it through the ownership confirmation, and reloaded to verify persisted history and cadence. Opened the category-scoped Rhythm editor and confirmed Friendships was selected in its category picker; canceled without saving. ## People-owned Friendships membership and connection-first Friends (2026-10-29)

**Authoritative membership and compatibility:** Friendships membership is now selected from the persisted People `friends` group by stable Person ID; cadence and the single connection-event array remain owned by Friendships. The local Friendships add-person picker and independent `friendIds` list were removed. Mark/unmark controls live on the existing People page. Unclassifying a person hides them from active Friends/Today projections without removing their cadence or events; reclassification restores the same identity and history. The v19-to-v20 migration transfers the saved v19 friend IDs exactly into the People Friends group, preserving cadence and connection history without adding sample memberships or inventing activity. The exact v19 source remains available through the versioned recovery path; migration/recovery and preview-envelope checks cover this transition.

**Friends experience and shared reuse:** `FriendshipsView` derives membership using the People-group selector, shows calm person rows with last connection and a quiet cadence chooser, and offers Text/Call/Time Together draft actions plus access to the shared connection history. Quick actions open the existing connection editor with the chosen type and only that friend selected; they do not save until explicit Save. History, edit, and confirmed delete operate on the authoritative event array, so all participant histories and cadence projections update from the same event. The People route reuses `PersonBadge`/`PersonBadgeVisual`; App supplies persisted People groups to Friendships and existing association pickers. `ConnectionEditor` remains the narrowly scoped connection-specific form; it reuses shared dialog framing and date/time controls. The shared `RhythmDetailsDialog`, `RhythmEditor`, and archive/history routes from the preceding section remain unchanged. No Foundation, scoring, Rhythm completion, duplicate membership, or second history store was introduced. Runtime tests protect behavior and integration contracts but do not statically enforce architecture or replace manual assistive-technology review.

## Shared People profile experience (2026-10-29)

**Implementation and authoritative data:** Replaced the profile placeholder with a modal shared by People rows, Rhythm person badges, and Friendships rows/history. Stable Person IDs scope both active/archived Rhythms (`personIds`) and recorded connections (the existing Friendship event array). People groups remain the sole source of classifications; only the existing Friends classification exposes cadence. Rhythms, connection events, and derived lists are not duplicated or persisted, and no schema or migration change was needed. Adding a person-linked Rhythm starts with that Person ID and no Category/Foundation association. Shared connection drafts keep participant selection explicit and update only the saved event's actual participants.

**Shared reuse map and duplication review:** `RhythmDetailsDialog` → Today, Marriage/Parenting, Friendships, and PersonProfileView; `RhythmEditor`/`ArchivedRhythmsDialog` → shared edit and archive/restore flows; `FriendshipConnectionControls` → FriendshipsView and PersonProfileView (quick actions, cadence, editor, history, delete confirmation); `PersonAvatar` → profile identity and PersonBadgeVisual. PersonProfileView also uses the existing score selectors and completion/history callbacks, category/Foundation badge components, dialog framing, and date/time controls. The repeated connection form/history implementation was extracted from FriendshipsView, and profile pages contain no local Rhythm-card implementation, second history store, or parallel profile-specific editor. Automated component/domain tests cover rendered contracts and behavior, but cannot statically prevent future duplicated markup/styles or replace assistive-technology review.

**Behavior and tests:** Added `PersonProfileView` tests for Person-ID scoping, linked/shared Rhythm cards, Tend/Edit/history/Undo controls, Add Rhythm preselection, archive/restore, connection draft defaults and Cancel, one-event persistence, actual-participant edits, delete behavior, preserved cadence, tab keyboard navigation, and Escape. App integration tests cover the same profile from People, Rhythm badges, and Friendships, person-only Rhythm draft/cancel behavior, and connection persistence/reload. The component-catalogue test now checks the shared profile and concise empty Connections state instead of the deleted placeholder. The catalogue profile uses in-memory synthetic data and intentionally disables Add Rhythm; its demonstration callbacks do not represent the live preview's persistence behavior.
